3144 AE5E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3145 AE60 0450  20 	b *r0
3146               	;
3147               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3148               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3149               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3150               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3151               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3152               	; 	'
3153               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3154               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3155               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3156               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3157               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3158               	; 	'
3159               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3160               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3161               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3162               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3163               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3164               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3165               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3166               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3167               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3168               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3169               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3170               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3171               	; 	' nothing in it.
3172               	; 	'
3173               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3174               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3175               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3176               	; 	'
3177               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3178               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3179               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3180               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3181               	; 	'
3182               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3183               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3184               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3185               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3186               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3187               	; 	'
3188               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3189               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3190               	; floor0_colour:
3191               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3192               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3193               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3194               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3195               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3196               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3197               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB
3198 AE62 0200  20 	li r0,24576   ; 96*256
     AE64 6000     
3199 AE66 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE68 20D2     
3200               	; 	f0v = 177			' LYELL on BLACK -- no ink, so the
3201 AE6A 0200  20 	li r0,45312   ; 177*256
     AE6C B100     
3202 AE6E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE70 20DA     
3203               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows			' background is all that shows
3204 AE72 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE74 A980     
3205 AE76 AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3206               	; 	f0c = CH_SLABE
3207 AE78 0200  20 	li r0,24832   ; 97*256
     AE7A 6100     
3208 AE7C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AE7E 20D2     
3209               	; 	f0v = 225			' GRAY brick on BLACK
3210 AE80 0200  20 	li r0,57600   ; 225*256
     AE82 E100     
3211 AE84 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AE86 20DA     
3212               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows
3213 AE88 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AE8A A980     
3214 AE8C AE92     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3215               	; 	RETURN
3216 AE8E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3217 AE90 0450  20 	b *r0
3218               	;
3219               	; 	' The last three scan lines of character f0c, in the BOTTOM SCREEN THIRD
3220               	; 	' only -- which is why this cannot be a DEFINE COLOR: that always writes
3221               	; 	' all three thirds (define_color takes the LDIRVM3 path unconditionally).
3222               	; f0_rows:
3223               cvb_F0_ROWS
3224               	; 	#f0a = 12288			' colour table, bottom screen third
3225 AE92 0200  20 	li r0,12288
     AE94 3000     
3226 AE96 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0A
     AE98 2134     
3227               	; 	#f0b = f0c
3228 AE9A D020  34 	movb @cvb_F0C,r0
     AE9C 20D2     
3229 AE9E 0980  32 	srl r0,8
3230 AEA0 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0B
     AEA2 2138     
3231               	; 	#f0b = #f0b + #f0b
3232 AEA4 A020  34 	a @cvb__F0B,r0
     AEA6 2138     
3233 AEA8 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__F0B
     AEAA 2138     
3234               	; 	#f0b = #f0b + #f0b
3235 AEAC A020  34 	a @cvb__F0B,r0
     AEAE 2138

3598               	;
3599               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3600               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3601               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3602               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3603               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3604               	; 	'
3605               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3606               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3607               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3608               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3609               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3610               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3611               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3612               	; 	'
3613               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3614               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3615               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3616               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3617               	; alock_cal:
3618               cvb_ALOCK_CAL

3734 B38E A656     	data wait
3735               	; 	' A KEY MUST BE HELD FOR THREE FRAMES TO COUNT.
3736               	; 	'
3737               	; 	' ALPHA LOCK shorts a keyboard line, and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps
3738               	; 	' so it reads DOWN with the host's Caps Lock UP -- the normal state of a
3739               	; 	' keyboard. cont1.key then returns values nobody pressed, a frame or two at
3740               	; 	' a time.
3741               	; 	'
3742               	; 	' That is fatal to the sequence below SPECIFICALLY BECAUSE its reset is
3743               	; 	' strict: anything that is not the next digit puts t838 back to 0, which is
3744               	; 	' what stops 8,5,3,8 opening the page. A spurious read BETWEEN the player's
3745               	; 	' own presses does exactly the same thing, so typing 8-3-8 cleared itself
3746               	; 	' halfway and appeared to do nothing -- and then typing 3-8 worked, because
3747               	; 	' the final 8 of the failed attempt was still standing as state. Reported
3748               	; 	' from play in precisely that shape.
3749               	; 	'
3750               	; 	' Three frames is 50 ms. A human press is several times that and the noise
3751               	; 	' is one or two, so the filter separates them cleanly. tkn caps AT the
3752               	; 	' threshold and the test is for equality, so a held key is accepted once
3753               	; 	' rather than on every frame it is down.
3754               	; 	tk = cont1.key

3894               cv94
3895               	; 	' FIRE ONLY, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.
3896               	; 	'
3897               	; 	' `1` used to start the game as well, because on the TI joystick fire is
3898               	; 	' TAB -- neither guessable nor forgiving, since Windows treats a stray TAB
3899               	; 	' as a focus change and moves the whole window away. CLAUDE.md 3A still
3900               	; 	' says not to make FIRE the only way out of a title screen, and this screen
3901               	; 	' is the named exception.
3902               	; 	'
3903               	; 	' WHAT IT BUYS IS THE 838 SEQUENCE. Every digit on this screen now means
3904               	; 	' exactly one thing: a step of the cheat code, or a reset of it. While `1`
3905               	; 	' also meant START, mistyping one digit of 8-3-8 did not merely reset the
3906               	; 	' sequence -- it began a game, which is a far worse outcome than having to
3907               	; 	' type the code again, and it is the single most likely typo since 1 is
3908               	; 	' next to nothing on the sequence but adjacent to everything on a keypad.
3909               	; 	'
3910               	; 	' The hazard the digit guarded against is real but recoverable: a TAB that
3911               	; 	' moves focus leaves the title on screen and the player clicks back. A
3912               	; 	' game started by accident cannot be undone at all.
3913               	; 	IF cont1.button THEN RETURN
3914 B4A4 D020  30 	movb @joy1_data,r0
     B4A6 8346     
3915 B4A8 0201  20 	li r1,16384   ; 64*256
     B4AA 4000

3917 B4AE 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
3918 B4B0 1602  14 	jne cv97
3919 B4B2 0460  28 	b @cv96
     B4B4 B4BA     
3920               cv97
3921 B4B6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3922 B4B8 0450  20 	b *r0
3923               cv96

3925 B4BA 0460  28 	b @cvb_TITLE_WAIT
     B4BC B38A     
3926               	;
3927               	; 	' 8-3-8 IS EDGE-TRIGGERED AND ANY STRAY DIGIT RESETS IT (see title_wait).
3928               	; 	'
3929               	; 	' It used to read cont1.key raw every pass and test only for the digit it
3930               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3931               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3932               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3933               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3934               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3935               	; 	'
3936               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3937               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3938               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3939               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3940               	; 	'
3941               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3942               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.
3943               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------- 838 setup page
3944               	; setup838:
3945               cvb_SETUP838
3946               	; 	CLS
3947 B4BE 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B4C0 A980     
3948 B4C2 A0FC     	data cls
3949               	; 	' ONE PROMPT AT A TIME, NOTHING ELSE ON THE SCREEN, AND NO NUMBERS UNTIL
3950               	; 	' THEY ARE TYPED. There is no heading and no instructions: a page showing
3951               	; 	' exactly one question does not need to explain that one digit answers it,
3952               	; 	' and the second question does not exist until the first is answered.
3953               	; 	'
3954               	; 	' Showing the CURRENT values first was the obvious thing and it cost more
3955               	; 	' than it gave -- two draw routines, a "fire keeps what is shown" escape to
3956               	; 	' make the display mean something, and the defaults had to be applied here
3957               	; 	' as well as in new_game so the page had numbers to show at all. None of it
3958               	; 	' survives: every digit on screen is one the player just typed. Four
3959               	; 	' strings became two, a two-field loop became a straight line, and the page
3960               	; 	' got SMALLER while getting quieter.

4278               	; start_krook:
4279               cvb_START_KROOK
4280               	; 	' Kelly starts MID-SCREEN on the first floor's east end. He used to start
4281               	; 	' at x 224, hard against the east wall at 232 -- the far end of the store
4282               	; 	' from floor 1's escalator, which made the climb three full traverses.
4283               	; 	'
4284               	; 	' Standing in the corner is a bad first frame: a quarter of the screen

4336               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked

4338               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4339               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that

4344               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4345               	; 	'

4347               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early

4352               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4353               	; 	'
4354               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,

4592 B91A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4593 B91C 0450  20 	b *r0
4594               	;
4595               	; 	' ======================================================================
4596               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4597               	; 	' ======================================================================
4598               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4599               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4600               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4601               	; 	' of running.
4602               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4603               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4604               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4605               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4606               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4607               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4608               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4609               	; 	'
4610               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4611               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4612               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4613               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4614               	; 	'
4615               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4616               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4617               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4618               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4619               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4620               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4621               	; 	'
4622               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4623               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4624               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4625               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4626               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4627               	; 	'
4628               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4629               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends

4643               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4644               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4645               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4646               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4647               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4648               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4649               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4650               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4651               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4652               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4741               	;
4742               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4743               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4744               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4745               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4746               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4747               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4748               	; 	'
4749               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4750               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4751               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4752               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4753               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4754               	; 	'
4755               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4756               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4757               	; 	' above it at all.
4758               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4759               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4760               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4761               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4762               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4763               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4764               	; 	'
4765               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4766               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4767               	; beam_one:
4768               cvb_BEAM_ONE

5210               	; 			' THE HAZARDS ARRIVE ONE PER KROOK, which is the original's

5212               	; 			'
5213               	; 			'   1  short beach balls, and nothing else
5214               	; 			'   2  + radios          5  balls go tall

5219               	; 			' than nothing, so a floor is never empty and Krook 1 is
5220               	; 			' the "short balls" screen the guides describe. This is
5221               	; 			' also why there are no biplanes on Krook 1: the one thing

5301               cv191
5302               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5303               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5304               	; 			' floor has one.
5305               	; 			'
5306               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5307               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor
5308               	; 			' empty, so that a floor is never bare -- and the effect on
5309               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5310               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5311               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5312               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5313               	; 			'
5314               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5315               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5316               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5317               	; 			'
5318               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5319               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5320               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do
5321               	; 			' not stack into a visibly dead region that reads as a bug
5322               	; 			' -- and the ROOF is last, because that is where the round
5323               	; 			' is decided.
5324               	; 			IF krk < 2 THEN
5325 BE5E D020  34 	movb @cvb_KRK,r0
     BE60 20B4

5365               	; 			'
5366               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5367               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5368               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5369               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5370               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5371               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5372               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5373               	; 			'
5374               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5375               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5376               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5377               	; 			'
5378               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5379               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5413               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5414               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5415               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5416               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5417               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5418               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.
5419               	; 			obc(li) = 0
5420 BEEC 04C1  14 	clr r1

     BEF0 21E0

     BEF6 2274     
5424 BEF8 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5425               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5426               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5427               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5428               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5429               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5430               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5431               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5432               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5433               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5434               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5435               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5436               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5437               	; 			'
5438               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5439               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5440               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5441               	; 			'
5442               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5443               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5444               	; 			'
5445               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5446               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5447               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5448               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5449               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5450               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5451               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5452               	; 			' reported from play.
5453               	; 			'
5454               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5455               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5456               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5457               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5458               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5459               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5460               	; 			'
5461               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5462               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5463               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5464               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5465               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5466               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5467               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5468               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5469               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5470               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5471               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5472               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5473               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5474               	; 			stag = lx AND 63

5555               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5556               	; 			'
5557               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5558               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5559               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5560               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5561               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5562               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5563               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5564               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5565               	; 			'
5566               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5567               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5568               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5569               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5570               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5571               	; 			'
5572               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5573               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5574               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5575               	; 			'
5576               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5577               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5578               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5579               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5580               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5581               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5582               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5583 BFBA D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BFBC 21EF     
5584 BFBE 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BFC0 1F00

6231               	;
6232               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
6233               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so
6234               	; 	' sixteen cells, and the three interesting things about a cell are all
6235               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
6236               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
6237               	; 	'
6238               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
6239               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
6240               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
6241               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
6242               	; 	' with nothing behind it.
6243               	; 	'
6244               	; 	' Nested IFs, never `ccl > 0 AND ccl < 3` -- the 9900 backend miscompiles
6245               	; 	' a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
6246               	; car_cell:

6253               	; 	' part-open one -- which the cst=1 branch below never touches. The step
6254               	; 	' is then continuous across all four columns of the opening on every
6255               	; 	' floor, including the three the car is not on. It spans the opening and
6256               	; 	' not the jambs: EJAMBL/EJAMBR sit in the wall column either side and

6470               cv299
6471               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6472               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6473               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6474               	; 		'
6475               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6476               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6477               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6478               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6479               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6480               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6481               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6482               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6483               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6484               	; 		'
6485               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6486               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6487               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6488               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6489               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6490               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6792               	;
6793               	; 	' --- running
6794               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6795               	; 	'
6796               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6797               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6798               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6799               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6800               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6801               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6802               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6803               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6804               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6805               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.
6806               	; 	IF klst = ST_JUMP THEN

     C952 21B2

7014               cv382
7015               	; 		' THE FIRST STEP OFF THE MARK IS ALWAYS HEARD. sfw is a distance --
7016               	; 		' a step every fifteen pixels -- so a short tap of the stick moved
7017               	; 		' him a few pixels and made NO sound at all. That does not read as
7018               	; 		' a short step, it reads as the controls being ignored, and it is
7019               	; 		' worst exactly where a player taps rather than holds: lining up a
7020               	; 		' jump, or edging around a hazard.
7021               	; 		'
7022               	; 		' Priming the accumulator to the threshold makes the very next pass
7023               	; 		' fire. It does not cheat the RATE: the trigger subtracts fifteen
7024               	; 		' rather than zeroing, so the pixels actually travelled still carry
7025               	; 		' into the following step and a held run comes out at 6.9 a second
7026               	; 		' either way.
7027               	; 		IF kmvl = 0 THEN sfw = 15
7028 CB06 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KMVL,r0
     CB08 2209

     CB16 220C

7035               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
7036               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
7037               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
7038               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
7039               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
7040               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
7041               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
7042               	; 		'
7043               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a
7044               	; 		' footstep is a stride rather than a tick.

7159               	;
7160               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7161               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7162               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7163               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7164               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7165               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7166               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7167               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7168               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7169               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7170               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7171               	; 	'
7172               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7173               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7174               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7175               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7176               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7212               	; 	' HOW FAR ALONG THE FLIGHT HE IS, measured from its head, in the flight's
7213               	; 	' own x. A step's centre sits at 8 x its index, so the bottom step (9)
7214               	; 	' owns 68..76, the one above it 60..68, and so on up. Both directions
7215               	; 	' land on the same numbers because the east flight is the west one
7216               	; 	' mirrored. The `+ escp` terms are the animation: the whole staircase
7217               	; 	' slides 2 px along and 1 px up per phase, and a rider who ignores that
7218               	; 	' boards up to 3 px out of register and drifts for the whole ride.

7735               	;
7736               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7737               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7738               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7739               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7740               	; 	'
7741               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7742               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7743               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7744               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7745               	; 	'
7746               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7747               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7748               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7749               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7750               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7751               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7752               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7753               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7754               	; 	'
7755               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7756               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7757               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7758               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7759               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7760               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7761               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7762               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7763               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7764               	; 	'
7765               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7766               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7767               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7768               	; wipe_shelf:
7769               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7770               	; 	wsn = 8

7842               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps

7844               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7845               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7846               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7847               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7848               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7849               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7850               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7851               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7852               	; 	' nothing.
7853               	; 	'
7854               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7855               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7856               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7857               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no

7913               	;
7914               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
7915               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
7916               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
7917               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
7918               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
7919               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7920               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7921               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
7922               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
7923               	; 	'

7925               	; 	' the position. COUNTR is a pillar or a counter; anything else stays.

8408               	; upd_obst:
8409               cvb_UPD_OBST
8410               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8411               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8412               	; 	'
8413               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8414               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8415               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8416               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8417               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8418               	; 	pacc = oacb

     D554 20F7     
8451               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8452               	; 	'
8453               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8454               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8455               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8456               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8457               	; 	'
8458               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8459               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8460               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8461               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8462               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to
8463               	; 	' clear it.
8464               	; 	pacc = obac

8679 D732 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8680 D734 0450  20 	b *r0
8681               	;
8682               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8683               	; 	'
8684               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8685               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8686               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8687               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8688               	; 	'
8689               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8690               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8691               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8692               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8693               	; 	'
8694               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8695               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8696               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8697               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8698               	; 	'

9190               cv583
9191               	;
9192               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9193               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9194               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9195               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9196               	; 	' runs every pass.
9197               	; 	'
9198               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9199               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9200               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9201               	; 	'
9202               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9203               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9204               	; 	'
9205               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9206               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9207               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9208               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9209               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9210               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9211               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9212               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9213               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9214               	; 	'
9215               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9216               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9217               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9218               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9219               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9220               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9221               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9222               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9223               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9224               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9225               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9226               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9227               	; 	'
9228               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9229               	; 	'
9230               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9231               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9232               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9233               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9234               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9235               	; 	'
9236               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9237               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9238               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9239               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9240               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

9321               cv594
9322               	;
9323               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9324               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9325               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9326               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9327               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9328               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9329               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9330               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9331               	; 	'
9332               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9333               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9334               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9335               	; 	'
9336               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9337               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9338               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9339               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9340               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9341               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9342               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9343               	; 	'
9344               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9345               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9346               	; 	'
9347               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9348               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9349               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9350               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9351               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9352               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9353               	; 	'
9354               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9355               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9356               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9357               	; 	' before any of this.
9358               	; 	'
9359               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9360               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9361               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9362               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9363               	; 	' journey.
9364               	; 	'
9365               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9366               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9367               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9368               	; 	pacc = hacc

     DB9C 21AD     
     DB9E 2122

9531               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
9532               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
9533               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
9534               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
9535               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
9536               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
9537               	; 	'
9538               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
9539               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any
9540               	; 	' speed, including the frames where the accumulator gives him nothing.

9801               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9802               	; 	' ======================================================================
9803               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9804               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9805               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9806               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9807               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9808               	; 	'
9809               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9810               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9811               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9812               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9813               	; 	' broken.
9814               	; 	'
9815               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9816               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9817               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9818               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9819               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9820               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9821               	; 	'
9822               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9823               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9824               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9825               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9826               	; coll_obst:
9827               cvb_COLL_OBST
9828               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the

10125               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10126               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10127               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10128               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10129               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10130               	; 	' away from.
10131               	; 	'
10132               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10133               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10134               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10135               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10136               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10137               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10138               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10139               	; 	'
10140               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10141               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10142               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10143               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
10144               	; 	GOSUB haz_gone
10145 E17E 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     E180 A980     
10146 E182 D378     	data cvb_HAZ_GONE
10147               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,
10148               	; 	' which dropped him straight down out of mid-air onto whatever he happened
10149               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
10150               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
10151               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
10152               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
10153               	; 	'
10154               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
10155               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.
10156               	; 	IF klst <> ST_JUMP THEN
10157 E184 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E186 21B2     
10158 E188 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E18A 0100

     E192 E1A6

10314               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
10315               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
10316               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
10317               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10318               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
10319               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
10320               	; 	'
10321               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
10322               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
10323               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
10324               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

10357               	; draw_actors:
10358               cvb_DRAW_ACTORS
10359               	; 	' RIDING WITH THE DOORS SHUT: HIDE HIM AND DO NOT DRAW HIM AT ALL.
10360               	; 	'
10361               	; 	' This test used to sit AFTER his sprites had been written, so every pass
10362               	; 	' placed him at his old floor and then hid him again. That is not free.
10363               	; 	' CVBasic's SPRITE writes a RAM mirror which the vblank ISR copies to
10364               	; 	' VRAM, so a vblank landing BETWEEN the draw and the hide latches the
10365               	; 	' visible state for a frame -- and draw_actors is long (Kelly, Harry,
10366               	; 	' eight obstacles) while a pass on the lift screen spans two or three
10367               	; 	' frames, so it happened constantly. He flickered at the floor he had
10368               	; 	' just left, which reads as a drawing bug rather than as a journey.
10369               	; 	'
10370               	; 	' Deciding BEFORE drawing costs one test and cannot race the ISR at all:
10371               	; 	' the mirror never holds a position to be caught with.
10372               	; 	IF klst = ST_ELEV THEN
10373 E31E D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E320 21B2

10450               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10451               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10452               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10453               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10454               	; 	'
10455               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10456               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10457               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10458               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10459               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10460               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10461               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10606               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10607               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10608               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10609               	; 		'
10610               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10611               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10612               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10613               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10614               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10615               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10616 E500 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E502 3800     
10617 E504 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E506 21DC     
10618               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10619               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10620               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10621               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10622               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10623               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10624               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.
10625               	; 		IF kanim AND 8 THEN kq = kq + 4

     E6F8 21C4     
10853               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10854               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10855               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10856               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10857               	; 	'
10858               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10859               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10860               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10861               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10862               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10863               	; 	'
10864               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10865               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10866               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10867               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10868               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10869               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10870               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10871               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10902               	; 	' are those same bands extended sideways, so the stripe drawing changes

10904               	; 	' any more (genart.py, over HARRY_STRIPE). Same bit of the counter, so the
10905               	; 	' two can never disagree.
10906               	; 	' Same bit as the body, necessarily -- the stripes ARE the body, drawn in
10907               	; 	' the other colour. Keyed to a different bit they would swing apart from
10908               	; 	' the shirt they belong to.

10912               	; 	' half a stride behind his shirt. Same reason hqs comes from hq.

10947               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10948               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10949               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10950               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10951               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10952               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10953               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10954               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10955               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10956               	; 	'
10957               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10958               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10959               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10960               	; 	' a jump.
10961               	; 	'
10962               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10963               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10964               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10965               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10966               	; 	'
10967               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10968               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10969               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10970               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10971               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10972               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10973               	; 	'
10974               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10975               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10976               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10977               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10978               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10979               	; 	'
10980               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10981               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10982               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10983               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10984               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

11168               	; 		' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON -- but
11169               	; 		' only while he is on THIS SCREEN. Once you are face to face the
11170               	; 		' round is a foot race you can see, and a ball arriving in the
11171               	; 		' middle of it costs nine seconds for reasons that have nothing to
11172               	; 		' do with the chase.
11173               	; 		'
11174               	; 		' THE SCREEN TEST IS WHAT MAKES IT INVISIBLE. Clearing his whole
11175               	; 		' floor regardless of screen meant the cleared band moved with him,
11176               	; 		' so every time he changed storeys one floor's hazards vanished and
11177               	; 		' another's appeared in the same instant -- and with all four bands
11178               	; 		' in view at once that reads as objects hopping between levels. Now
11179               	; 		' it only happens on the screen he is actually on, where the reason
11180               	; 		' for it is standing right there.
11181               	; 		'

11452 EB82 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11453 EB84 0450  20 	b *r0
11454               	;
11455               	; 	' ======================================================================
11456               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11457               	; 	' ======================================================================
11458               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11459               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11460               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11461               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11462               	; 	'
11463               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11464               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11465               	; 	'
11466               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11467               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11468               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11469               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11470               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11471               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11472               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11473               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11474               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11475               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11476               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11477               	; esc_tick:
11478               cvb_ESC_TICK
11479               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11480               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11481               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11482               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11483               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11484 EB86 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB88 219E     
11485 EB8A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EB8C 0100     
11486 EB8E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11487 EB90 1402  14 	jhe cv809
11488 EB92 0460  28 	b @cv808
     EB94 EBAA     
11489               cv809
11490               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11491 EB96 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB98 219E     
11492 EB9A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EB9C 0700     
11493 EB9E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11494 EBA0 1A02  14 	jl cv811
11495 EBA2 0460  28 	b @cv810
     EBA4 EBAA     
11496               cv811
11497 EBA6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11498 EBA8 0450  20 	b *r0
11499               cv810
11500               	; 	END IF
11501               cv808
11502               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11503               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11504               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11505               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11506               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11507               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11508               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11509               	; 	'
11510               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11511               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11512               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11513               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11514               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11515               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11516               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11517               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11518 EBAA D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EBAC 20B3     
11519 EBAE 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EBB0 0100     
11520 EBB2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EBB4 20B3     
11521               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
11522 EBB6 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EBB8 0400     
11523 EBBA 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11524 EBBC 1402  14 	jhe cv813
11525 EBBE 0460  28 	b @cv812
     EBC0 EBC8     
11526               cv813
11527 EBC2 04C0  14 	clr r0
11528 EBC4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EBC6 20B3     
11529               cv812
11530               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11531               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11532               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11533               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11534               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11535               	; 	'
11536               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11537               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11538               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11539               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11540               	; 	'
11541               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11542               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11543               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11544               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11545               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
