11813               	;
11814               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11815               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11816               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11817               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11818               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11819               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11820               	; 	'
11821               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11822               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11823               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11824               	; 	'
11825               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11826               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11827               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11828               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11829               	; 	'
11830               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11831               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11832               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11833               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11834               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11835               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11836               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11837               	; 	'
11838               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11839               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11840               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11841               	; 	'
11842               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11843               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11844               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11845               	; scan_furn:
11846               cvb_SCAN_FURN
11847               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3

11887               	; 		' THE ESCALATOR DIAGONALS USED TO BE DRAWN HERE TOO, and it was dead
11888               	; 		' work: scan_escs runs immediately below and draws the same three rows
11889               	; 		' for the same three floors. Every input is a pure function of fl --
11890               	; 		' scan_base, the side lookup, the masks, scan_pat -- and scan_or1 is an
11891               	; 		' OR, so doing it twice cannot differ from doing it once. Sixteen
11892               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing
11893               	; 		' on screen could reflect.
11894               	; 		'
11895               	; 		' It is the shape a routine takes when a feature is split out and the
11896               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour
11897               	; 		' write that was added later, and it carried a copy of the explanatory
11898               	; 		' comment as well as the code.
11899               	; 		'
11900               	; 		' A SCREENSHOT COULD NOT HAVE SETTLED IT: the radar is three pixel rows
11901               	; 		' tall and Classic99 clips the bottom of the screen. It was proved by
11902               	; 		' replaying both versions against a model of the pattern table for all
11903               	; 		' eight escalator-side combinations.
11904               	; 		WAIT
11905 EE66 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     EE68 A980

12184 F09A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12185 F09C 0450  20 	b *r0
12186               	;
12187               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12188               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12189               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12190               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12191               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12192               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12193               	; scan_wipe:
12194               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12195               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12196               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12197               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12198               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12199               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12200               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12201               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12202               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12203               	; 	'
12204               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12205               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12206               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12207               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12208               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12209               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12210               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12211               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12212               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12213               	; 	'
12214               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12215               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12216               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12217               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12218               	; 	'
12219               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12220               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12221               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12222 F09E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F0A0 1680     
12223 F0A2 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     F0A4 20E4

12289               	;
12290               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12291               	; 	'
12292               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12293               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12294               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12295               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12296               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12297               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12298               	; 	'
12299               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12300               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12301               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12302               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12303               	; 	'
12304               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12305               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12306               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12307               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12308               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12309               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12310               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12311               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12312               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12313               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12314               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
12315               	; 	say = 3 - elvl

13024               	;
13025               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13026               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13027               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13028               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13029               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13030               	; 	'
13031               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13032               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13033               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
13034               	; 	'
13035               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
13036               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
13037               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
13038               	; 	'
13039               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
13040               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13041               	; bonus_count:

     F7A0 F6F2     
13144               	;
13145               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13146               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13147               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13148               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13149               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13150               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13151               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13152               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13153               	; 	'
13154               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13155               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13156               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13157               	;
13158               	; do_escape:
13159               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13160               	; 	escapd = 0
13161 F7A2 04C0  14 	clr r0
13162 F7A4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCAPD
     F7A6 2116     
13163               	; 	rsn = 0
13164 F7A8 04C0  14 	clr r0

     F7AC 220E

     F7B0 F7DE

13198               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13199               	; 	'
13200               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13201               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13202               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13203               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13204               	; 	'
13205               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13206               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13207               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13208               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
13209               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
13210               	; 	'
13211               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
13212               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
13213               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
13214               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
13215               	; 	'
13216               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
13217               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
13218               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
13219               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
13220               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
13221               	; 	' program uses a constant.
13222               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "

     F7E6 014A

13385               	;
13386               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13387               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13388               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13389               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13390               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13391               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13392               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13393               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13394               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off

13545               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13546               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13547               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13548               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13549               	; 	'
13550               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13551               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13552               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13553               	; 	'
13554               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13555               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13556               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13557               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13558               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.

13728               cv984
13729               	;
13730               	; 	' the jump sweep: divisor falling = pitch rising
13731               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
13732               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
13733               	; 	'
13734               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
13735               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
13736               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
13737               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
13738               	; 	' recording shows.
13739               	; 	'
13740               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
13741               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
13742               	; 	' is written).
13743               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN
13744 FC18 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FC1A 2096

     FC1E 0100

13800               	;
13801               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
13802               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
13803               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
13804               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
13805               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
13806               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
13807               	;
13808               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
13809               	; 	'
13810               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
13811               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
13812               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
13813               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
13814               	; 	'
13815               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
13816               	; 	'
13817               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
13818               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
13819               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
13820               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
13821               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
13822               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
13823               	; 	'
13824               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
13825               	; 	'
13826               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
13827               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
13828               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
13829               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
13830               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
13831               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
13832               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
13833               	; 	'
13834               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
13835               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
13836               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
13837               	; 	' carry is wrong.
13838               	; 	'
13839               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
13840               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
13841               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
13842               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
13843               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
13844               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
13845               	; 	'
13846               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
13847               	; 	'
13848               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
13849               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
13850               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
13851               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
13852               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
13853               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
13854               	; 	' back to 7.
13855               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN
13856 FCA6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FCA8 220C     
13857 FCAA 0201  20 	li r1,3840   ; 15*256
     FCAC 0F00     
13858 FCAE 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
13859 FCB0 1402  14 	jhe cv993
13860 FCB2 0460  28 	b @cv992
     FCB4 FCEC     
13861               cv993
13862               	; 		sfw = sfw - 15
13863 FCB6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FCB8 220C     
13864 FCBA 0220  22 	ai r0,61696	; -15
     FCBC F100     
13865 FCBE D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SFW
     FCC0 220C     
13866               	; 		' STILL SILENT DURING THE JUMP, but now that is a CHOICE rather than
13867               	; 		' a channel conflict -- he is off the ground, so there is nothing to
13868               	; 		' make a footstep with.
13869               	; 		IF swt = 0 THEN
13870 FCC2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FCC4 2096

13872 FCC8 0460  28 	b @cv994
     FCCA FCEC

     FCD8 0900

     FCE0 A980

     FCEA 2228

13978               	;
13979               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13980               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13981               	; 	'
13982               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13983               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13984               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13985               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13986               	; 	'
13987               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13988               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13989               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13990               	; 	'
13991               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13992               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13993               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13994               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13995               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13996               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13997               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13998               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13999               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14000               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14001               	; 	#if TI994A
14002               	; 	BANK 1
14003      0258     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

14014               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
14015               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
14016               	; 	'
14017               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
14018               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
14019               	; 	' letter after it.

14142               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
14143               	; 	'
14144               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
14145               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
14146               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
14147               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
14148               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
14149               	; 	'
14150               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
14151               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.

14273               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14274               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14275               	; 	'
14276               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14277               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14278               	; 	' being small sprites.
14279               	; 	'
14280               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14281               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14282               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14283               	; 	'
14284               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14285               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14286               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14287               	;
14288               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14289               cvb_SPR_KELLY

     63E0 0000

15286               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15287               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15288               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15289               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15290               	;
15291               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15292               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15293               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15294               	; 	' unreadable.
15295               	;
15296               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

15408               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15409               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15410               	; 	'
15411               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15412               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15413               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15414               	; 	'
15415               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15416               	; 	' blit is
15417               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15418               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15419               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15420               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15421               	; 	'
15422               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15423               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15424               	; 	'
15425               	; 	' template ids:
15426               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15427               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15428               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15429               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15430               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15431               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15432               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15433               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15434               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15435               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15436               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15437               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15438               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15439               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15440               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15441               	;
15442               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15443               cvb_STOR_TPL
15444               	; 	DATA BYTE 154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154

     72DA 9A9A     
     72DC 6B9A     
     72DE 9A9A
